Cockpit Door Fault
CKPT DOOR CONT [OVHD PANEL]
CHECK
If one or more STRIKE status lights on:
COCKPIT DOOR  OPEN
COCKPIT DOOR
UNLOCK  10  THEN NORM
If two or more STRIKE status lights on:
COCKPIT DOOR NOT INTRUSION PROOF.
If two CHAN status lights on:
AUTOMATIC
LATCH RELEASE NOT AVAILABLE AFTER COCKPIT DECOMPRESSION.
If no status light on:
TO UNLOCK THE DOOR: COCKPIT DOOR HANDLE AVAIL

COCKPIT DOOR
UNLOCK  10  THEN NORM
If two or more STRIKE status lights on:
COCKPIT DOOR NOT INTRUSION PROOF.

UP

is not available when the  BCU  controls the  CDLS , it is recommended that at least two crewmembers remain in the cockpit
during that time.

Landing with Slats or Flaps Jammed
LDG DIST PROC  APPLY
Determine flap lever
position for landing.
Repeat the following until landing configuration is reached:
SPD SEL  VFE NEXT -  5
AT VFE NEXT: SELECT
FLAPS LEVER ONE STEP DOWN
overspeed
alert, and  VLS  displayed on the  PFD , are computed
according to the current flaps/slats position
VFE  and  VFE  NEXT are displayed on the  PFD  according
to the FLAPS lever position. If not displayed, use the placard speeds
In some cases,
the recommended speed for go-around requested by the procedure might
be slightly above the  VFE  displayed on  PFD  as
the  VFE  is linked to the  S/F  lever position.
The
overspeed
alert will not be triggered as it is taking into account
the current slat/flap position.
If  VLS  is greater than  VFE  NEXT (overweight landing case), the
FLAPS lever can be set in the required next position, while the speed
is reduced to follow  VLS  reduction as surfaces extend.
The  VFE  warning threshold should not be triggered.  In this case, disconnect
the  A/THR .  A/THR  can be re-engaged when the
landing configuration is established.

DECELERATE TO CALCULATED
VAPP

use and keep the selected speed.

Rudder Jam
LDG DIST PROC  APPLY
For approach:
AVOID
LANDING WITH CROSSWIND FROM THE SIDE WHERE THE RUDDER IS DEFLECTED
MAX XWIND
FOR LDG:  15
AUTO BRK  DO NOT USE
FOR LANDING  USE NORMAL CONF
SPEED AND TRAJECTORY  STABILIZE ASAP
For landing:
DIFFERENTIAL BRAKING  USE ASAP
REVERSER: SYMMETRIC
USE ONLY
Use  nosewheel steering  below  70 .
Stabilizer Jam
AP  OFF
MAN PITCH TRIM  CHECK
The pitch trim wheel may not
be fully jammed, the force needed may be higher than usual.
If  MAN  PITCH TRIM available:
TRIM FOR NEUTRAL ELEV
If manual pitch trim
is available, trim to maintain the elevator at the zero position (indications
on  F/CTL ).
If  MAN  PITCH TRIM not available:
FOR LDG  FLAP LVR 3
GPWS LDG FLAP 3  ON
CAT 1 ONLY
Fuel Leak
WHEN A LEAK IS CONFIRMED
Leak from engine/pylon confirmed by excessive fuel flow, low  N1 , or visual check:
THR LEVER (affected engine)  IDLE
ENG MASTER (affected engine)  OFF
FUEL X FEED  AS RQRD
DO NOT RESTART AFFECTED
ENGINE
Leak from engine/pylon not confirmed or leak not located:
FUEL X FEED  MAINTAIN CLOSED
CTR TK PUMP 1  OFF
CTR TK PUMP 2  OFF
INNER TANK FUEL QUANTITIES  MONITOR
If one inner tank depletes faster than other by at least  300  ( 660 ) in less than  30 :
THR LEVER (engine on leaking side)  IDLE
ENG MASTER (engine on leaking side)  OFF
CTR TK PUMP 1  ON
CTR TK PUMP 2  ON
FUEL LEAK  MONITOR
If leak stops:
ENGINE LEAK CONFIRMED
FUEL X FEED  AS RQRD
DO NOT RESTART AFFECTED
ENGINE
If leak continues (after engine shutdown):
WING LEAK SUSPECTED
ENGINE RESTART  CONSIDER
Do not apply the FUEL
IMBALANCE procedure. Approach and landing can be done, even with one

ENGINE

ENG 1(2) STALL
On Ground:
THR  LEVER (affected engine)  IDLE
ENG  MASTER (affected engine)  OFF
In Flight:
THR  LEVER (affected engine)  IDLE
ENG  PARAMETERS (affected engine)  CHECK
If abnormal  ENG  parameters:
ENG  MASTER (affected engine)  OFF
If the flight crew suspects
that the stall was transient, they can consider an engine relight.
If normal  ENG  parameters:
ENG  ANTI-ICE (affected engine)  ON
THR  LEVER (affected engine)  SLOWLY
MOVE FORWARD
If stall recurs:
THR  LEVER (affected engine)  MOVE
BACKWARD

fire.

When aircraft on ground and stopped in a remote area (if possible):
.
C/B Tripped
On ground:
Do not reengage the circuit breaker ( C/B ) of the fuel pump(s) of any tank. For all other  C/B , if the flight crew coordinates the action with maintenance, the
flight crew may reengage a tripped  C/B , provided that the
cause is identified.
In flight:
Do not reengage a circuit breaker ( C/B ), unless the captain judges it necessary to do so for the
safe continuation of the flight. Only one reengagement should be attempted.
System Reset - General
To know if a system reset is authorized,  the flight crew applies the following guidelines:
In Flight
If  ECAM / OEB / FCOM / QRH  procedure requests
a reset,
or
If the  ECAM  alert or the system malfunction is  listed and authorized  in the system reset
table
Reset authorized
On Ground
If  ECAM / OEB / FCOM / QRH  procedure requests
a reset,
or
If the  ECAM  alert or the system malfunction is  listed and authorized  in the system reset
table,
or
If the affected
system is  NOT listed  in the system reset
table.
Reset authorized
In all other cases:
RESET NOT AUTHORIZED.
In the case of several failures
related to the same system, RESET IS NOT AUTHORIZED if at least one
reset is not authorized.
To perform a reset,  unless there
is a specific reset procedure defined in the  ECAM / FCOM / QRH  or in the system reset table, the flight
crew applies the following  standard
reset procedure:
Only perform one reset
at a time, unless indicated differently in the applicable reset procedure
of the table.
Reset  ECU  (CFM56)
or  EEC  (IAE/PW/LEAP-1A) or  EIU  only when both
engines are shut down.

ECAM Advisory Conditions - 70 - Engines
SYSTEM
CONDITIONS
RECOMMENDED ACTION
ENG
OIL PRESS  P  <  amber
threshold value
If the oil pressure
indication is amber, continue normal engine operation.
If the oil pressure
indication becomes red without the
ENG 1(2)  OIL LO
PR
alert, continue normal engine operation.
In both cases, monitor
other engine parameters, particularly oil temperature and oil quantity.
OIL PRESS  P  >
259
Closely monitor other
engine parameters for symptoms of engine malfunction.  If there is high oil
pressure without other abnormal indications, operate engine normally
for remainder of the flight..
Record high oil pressure,
and corresponding  N2  readings, for maintenance action.
OIL  TEMP
Oil temperature above
the advisory threshold and below the caution threshold.
An increase in oil
temperature during normal steady-state operation indicates a system
malfunction and should be closely monitored for other symptoms of
engine malfunction.
If the OIL  TEMP  increases at thrust reduction, consider increasing the thrust again
in order to attempt to lower the oil temperature.
In addition, an increase
in oil temperature could be related to the  IDG  oil cooling
system. To reduce oil temperature, before limits are reached, the
following are recommended:
Low Speed  - Increase engine speed to increase fuel flow and
thereby cool  IDG  oil.
High Speed  - Reduce generator load or turn off generator.
If the oil temperature continues to rise, mechanically disconnect  IDG .
OIL  QTY
<  1.45
QT
The oil quantity in
the tank can decrease at high thrust setting due to the effect of
oil gulping. In that case, the indicated oil quantity will increase
after thrust reduction.  Monitor the affected
engine oil parameters and crosscheck with the other engine. As long
as the oil temperature and the oil pressure of the affected engine
remain within limits, normal engine operation is not affected.  If the oil quantity
continues to decrease, both of the following  ECAM  alerts
can be triggered:
ENG 1(2)  OIL LO PR
caution
ENG 1(2)  OIL LO PR
warning.
NAC  TEMP
≥
280
Monitor engine parameters
and crosscheck with other engine.
ECAM Advisory Conditions - ENG
SYSTEM
CONDITIONS
RECOMMENDED ACTION
ENG
OIL PRESS  P  <
80
If oil pressure
is between  80  and  60  continue normal
engine operation.
If oil pressure
is below  60  (red indication), without the
ENG 1(2)  OIL LO PR
alert, continue normal
engine operation (it can be assumed that the oil pressure transducer
is faulty).
In both cases, monitor
other engine parameters, especially oil temperature and oil quantity.

390

engine parameters for symptoms of engine malfunction. If there is
high oil pressure without other abnormal indications, operate engine
normally for remainder of the flight. Record high oil pressure, and
corresponding  N2  readings, for maintenance action.

T  >
155

increase during normal steady-state operations indicates a system
malfunction, and should be closely monitored for other symptoms of
engine malfunction.

TEMP
increase follows thrust reduction,

IDG

IDG

1
DH  INDICATION
1 for  PM
For automatic rollout, one
is required. For autoland without automatic rollout, none is required.
GLS  autoland is certified
for  CAT  I minima, but the  FMGS  monitors the
same equipments as for  CAT  2.
Electrical power supply split:
This ensures that each  FMGC  is powered by an independent
electrical source ( GEN  1 or  GEN  2).
Failure of antiskid and/or nosewheel
steering mechanical parts are not monitored for landing capability.
DH  will be displayed
on the  FMA , and ″Hundred Above″ and ″Minimum″ auto callouts
will be announced, provided that the  DH  value has been
entered on the  MCDU .
Flight  crews
 are  not  expected  to  check  the  equipment  list before approach.
When an  ECAM  or local caution occurs, the crew should use
the list to confirm the landing capability.
On  ground,
 the  equipment  list  determines  which  approach category the aircraft
will be able to perform at the next landing.

General Information - Page Numbering
Page Numbering
The page numbering follows the following
rules:
01A, 02A, 02B,..
Numbering and Index (A, B, ...)
for GEN, ABN, OPS, OEB PROC sections
For these sections, the procedures
start with the index A and for long procedures (more than one page),
the index continues with B, C...
1/10, 3/5, ...
Numbering for NP, PER
C1, C2
Back cover page interior
C3
Back cover page exterior
"BLANK"
Index of an intentionally left
blank paper page created to ensure the correct format of the next
chapter (begins on recto page)
General Information - Effectivity
Effectivity
As  QRH  is published
at aircraft level, each paper page has only one effectivity.
General Information - Preliminary Pages within the QRH Binder
Preliminary Pages within the QRH binder
It is essential for Airlines
to correctly manage the updates of the  QRH . For this purpose,
Airbus publishes Preliminary Pages ( PLP ) with each  QRH  revision. These  PLP  are used as reference documents
for Airlines to manage the  QRH  updates, e.g. easily insert
the revisions,  identify the modifications that impact the  QRH , get a synthesis of changes introduced with each revision. However,
when the  QRH  revisions have been incorporated in accordance
with the information given in the  PLP , these pages do not
bring operational added value and therefore are no longer useful in
the  QRH  binder for any operational purposes. Therefore,
to minimize the size of the  QRH  binder on board the aircraft
and to optimize the operational use of the  QRH , Airbus
has no objection that the Airlines remove the  PLP  from
the  QRH  after the revisions have been incorporated in the  QRH  and all checks performed to confirm the revisions have
been correctly incorporated.  You will find below the list of  PLP  that may be removed from the  QRH  binder :
The transmittal
letter
The List of
Effective Preliminary Pages ( LEPP )
The Filing Instructions
( FI )
The List of
Effective Documentary Units (the  LESS  is the reference)
The List Of
Modifications ( LOM )
The Summary
Of Highlights ( SOH )
The front pages
of all  QRH  sections
The Table Of
Contents ( TOC ) of the General section
The  OEB  General Description page 99.01A.
Use of Fuel Penalty Factor Tables
Use of the Fuel Penalty Factors
The Fuel Penalty Factors provided in
the following tables are conservative values, given as a guideline
in order to increase the crew awareness and to help the decision making.
In case of failure impacting
the fuel consumption, the fuel predictions provided by the  FMS  are no longer reliable (except in One Engine Inoperative OEI condition).
The flight crew must still compute and monitor the actual fuel consumption.
Refer to the following tables
in order to assess the impact of the failure on the fuel consumption
after any  ECAM  alert that:
Displays the
line
INCREASED FUEL
CONSUMP
or
FUEL CONSUMPT INCRSD
in the  STATUS , or
Displays Flight
Control Surfaces in the INOP SYS, or
Impacts the
Landing Gears or Landing Gear Doors retraction (when extended).
The Fuel Penalty Factors given
in these tables have been calculated taking into account:
The FUEL CRITICAL
INOP SYS, and
The aircraft
configuration, speed or altitude described in the CONDITIONS column.
Ensure
that all these conditions are well met before applying the corresponding
Fuel Penalty Factor.
Methodology
The methodology is the following:
Check the  ECAM ALERT table  to determine if a Fuel Penalty Factor is
applicable depending on the CONDITIONS column, then
Check the  INOP SYS table  in order to determine if, according to the
actual aircraft status, there is a Fuel Penalty Factor applicable
depending on the CONDITIONS column
If only one
Fuel Penalty Factor (FPF) is applicable:  TRIP FUEL PENALTY
= (FOB - EFOB at DEST) x FPF  The  FMS  fuel predictions must be recomputed to take into account this trip
fuel penalty.
If two or more
Fuel Penalty Factors (FPF) are applicable:  TRIP FUEL PENALTY
= (FOB - EFOB at DEST) x (FPF1 + FPF2 +...)  The  FMS  fuel predictions must be recomputed to take into account this trip
fuel penalty.
Due to previous failures in
flight or dispatch under  MEL , some failures could have
an impact on the fuel consumption:
Without being
mentioned in the  ECAM  ALERT table (only through INOP SYS
table), or
If mentioned
in the  ECAM  ALERT table, with additional INOP SYS (other
than the one(s) described in the FUEL CRITICAL INOP SYS column for
this specific  ECAM  alert) impacting also the fuel consumption.
Example:
Dispatch with
the  ELAC  1 inoperative under  MEL
HYD  G SYS LO PR
ECAM  caution in flight
These two failures
lead to the loss of the left aileron
INOP SYS will
displayed “  L AIL  ”
If the
Fuel Penalty Factor of the
HYD  G SYS LO PR
ECAM  alert is applicable (spoiler extended),
sum the corresponding factor with the Fuel Penalty Factor related
to the INOP SYS “  L(R) AIL  ” partially extended.  FPF (  HYD  G SYS LO PR  ) =  10
FPF (INOP SYS: L AIL) =  8
Therefore,
TRIP FUEL PENALTY = (FOB - EFOB at DEST) x ( 10  +  8 )
If the
Fuel Penalty Factor of the
HYD  G SYS LO PR
ECAM  alert is not applicable (spoiler remains
retracted), apply the Fuel Penalty Factor related to the INOP SYS
“  L(R) AIL  ” partially extended.  Therefore,
TRIP FUEL PENALTY = (FOB - EFOB at DEST) x  8

During the flight, the spoiler(s)
may gradually extend and increase(s) the fuel consumption.
A spoiler can be suspected fully
extended (runaway) if high roll rate has been experienced immediately
after the failure, associated with a possible AP disconnection. A
visual inspection, if time permits, can also confirm the full extension
of the spoiler.
The maximum value of the Fuel
Penalty Factor provided in the table considers that the two pairs
of corresponding spoilers gradually extend during the flight.
The minimum value of the Fuel
Penalty Factor provided in the table considers that all spoilers remain
retracted. The maximum value has been calculated considering that
all impacted spoilers gradually extend during the flight.

affected side:

If unsuccessful:
LAPTOP DOCKING STATION  OFF THEN ON

LAPTOP  UNPLUG, UNSTOW & VERIFY
If laptop affected:
LAPTOP  OFF THEN START
If successful:
LAPTOP  STOW & PLUG
If unsuccessful:
LAPTOP DOCKING STATION  OFF

AS  RQRD

CONSIDER LAPTOP AS
STANDALONE

General Information - QRH Revision Date
QRH Revision Date
The update of the  FCOM  does not necessarily result in the update of the  QRH .
Therefore, the revision dates of the  QRH  and of the  FCOM  may differ.
Smoke / Fire from Lithium Battery
If necessary,
transfer control to the flight crewmember seated on the opposite side
of the fire.
CKPT / CAB COM  ESTABLISH
STORAGE AFTER Li BAT FIRE cabin procedure  REQUEST
INITIATION

WATER or NON-ALCOHOLIC LIQUID  POUR ON DEVICE
DEVICE  MONITOR
If possible to remove device from cockpit:
DEVICE  TRANSFER TO CABIN

THREAT:
REMOVAL OF SMOKE / FUMES procedure  CONSIDER
.

Aircraft Configuration Summary Foreword
For awareness and for the specified aircraft,
the following table provides the flight crew with a list of optional
aircraft systems and functions related to aircraft flight operations.

FLYING TECHNIQUE
TO STABILIZE SPEED
Stabilize the altitude. When
altitude is stabilized:
If the pitch
is above the target pitch, increase the thrust and maintain the altitude.
If the pitch
is below the target pitch, decrease the thrust and maintain the altitude.
When the pitch reaches the target pitch,
adjust the thrust to keep this target pitch.

Performance impact
of one burst tire is equivalent to one brake released.

LG Landing Gear - Taxi with Deflated or Damaged Tires.
02 - Relight
If engine relight can be attempted:
ENG MODE sel  IGN
Approaching or below FL 300: Windmill Relight
ALL ENG MASTERS  OFF 30 S THEN ON
ENGs RELIGHT  TRY REGULARLY
Windmill relight attempts can
be repeated until successful, or until the  APU  bleed is
available.
APU  (below FL 250)  START
If  APU  available and windmill relight not successful
: Starter Assisted Relight below FL 200

General Description
OEB
Each  OEB  has an “ OEB
PROC ” that includes all of the following items:
The title of
the  OEB
PROC ,
The “ ECAM  ENTRY" field:  This section identifies
if one of the possible conditions to apply the  OEB
PROC  is an  ECAM  warning/caution.  The flight crew must
disregard the  ECAM  procedure and/or STATUS of the  ECAM  alerts listed in the " ECAM  ENTRY"
field and must apply the  QRH ’s  OEB  procedure
instead.
The  OEB  operational procedure(s) that the flight crew must apply
The technical
explanation of the deviation from the initial design objectives.
List of Effective OEB
The List of Effective Operations Engineering
Bulletins ( LEOEB ) enables the flight crew to check all
the  OEB s that are applicable to the aircraft MSN they operate.
The  LEOEB  provides:  The "Identification" field
that identifies the  OEB  number and its issue number.  The identification number and title of red  OEB s are in
bold font. The identification number and title of white  OEB s are in regular font. This identification field enables the flight
crew to easily check the  OEB s before flight.
OEB s are
listed in numerical order regardless of the type of OEB (red or white).
The "Title" field
provides the  OEB  title and the " ECAM Entry " part of the OEB PROC. This enables the flight
crew to easily identify the  ECAM  procedure that is affected
by the  OEB .
When Airbus provides
the Operator with the  LEOEB , the information “ ECAM Entry  ” does not necessarily mean that
(for Operators that use the  OEB  REMINDER function) the
Operator’s maintenance personnel  activated  the
OEB
REMINDER  codes  for  this
OEB
onboard  the aircraft.  It is the Operator’s
responsibility to define an appropriate process to provide the flight
crew with confirmation that the  OEB  REMINDER codes are
activated for the  ECAM  alerts affected by  OEB s.
A vertical bar in the margin of the  LEOEB  identifies that the  OEB  is either new, revised,
or has an aircraft validity change compared to the most recent published  LEOEB .

FLYING TECHNIQUE TO STABILIZE SPEED
Stabilize the altitude. When
altitude is stabilized:
If the pitch
is above the target pitch, increase the thrust and maintain the altitude
If the pitch
is below the target pitch, decrease the thrust and maintain the altitude.
When the pitch reaches the target pitch, adjust the thrust to keep
this target pitch.
When flight path is stabilized:
FLIGHT PATH  KEEP STABILIZED
RELIABLE SPEED IDENTIFICATION
PROBE/WINDOW HEAT  ON
ALL SPEED INDICATIONS (INCLUDE BKUP SPD)  CROSSCHECK
ADR 3 and  STBY  speeds use the data of the same probe.
If at least one speed confirmed reliable:
RELIABLE SPEED  USE
If  BKUP
SPD  is used:
MAX SPEED  280/.78
KEEP ONE  ADR  ON

OFF

use of data from two  ADR s that are not reliable for the
flight control laws.

TWO  ADR
OFF

from two  ADR s that are not reliable for the flight control

FLYING TECHNIQUE TO STABILIZE SPEED
Stabilize the altitude. When
altitude is stabilized:
If the pitch
is above the target pitch, increase the thrust and maintain the altitude
If the pitch
is below the target pitch, decrease the thrust and maintain the altitude.
When the pitch reaches the target pitch, adjust the thrust to keep
this target pitch.
When flight path is stabilized:
FLIGHT PATH  KEEP STABILIZED
RELIABLE SPEED IDENTIFICATION
PROBE/WINDOW HEAT  ON
ALL SPEED INDICATIONS (INCLUDE BKUP SPD)  CROSSCHECK

If at least one speed confirmed reliable:
RELIABLE SPEED  USE
If  BKUP
SPD  is used:
MAX SPEED  280/.78

from two  ADR s that are not reliable for the flight control
laws.

ENG 1(2) SYSTEM FAULT - Approval
Issue 1:
This  OEB  is issued to provide
operational recommendations in the case of
ENG 1(2)  SYSTEM
FAULT
ECAM  alert.
All A320neo PW aircraft.
Head of Airbus Flight Operations
and Training Support  2021-05-18T00:00:00  2021-05-18
Head of Flight Operations and Training Support
ENG 1(2) SYSTEM FAULT
ECAM Entry
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM FAULT
Procedure
Apply the following procedure
IN FLIGHT only:
During an engine relight, if
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM
FAULT
alert triggers:
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM FAULT
ECAM  procedure  APPLY
In all other cases:
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM FAULT
ECAM  alert  DISREGARD
ENG 1(2) SYSTEM FAULT - Explanation
Root cause
The
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM
FAULT
ECAM  alert can be triggered
due to:
Active Clearance
Control ( ACC ) valve fault, or
High Pressure
Compressor ( HPC ) bleed valve fault.
In flight,
it is not possible to identify if the
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM
FAULT
ECAM  alert is due to an  ACC  valve fault or an  HPC  bleed valve fault.  An  HPC  bleed valve can only fail open during an engine
relight.  An  ACC  valve may fail at any
time during the flight but it does not result in any engine deterioration
or aircraft operation impact.
Operational consequence
The
ENG  1(2) SYSTEM FAULT
ECAM  procedure leads the flight crew to reduce
thrust lever to idle level on the affected engine.

AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED ABNORM PR and  AIR  PACK 2(1) REGUL FAULT
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
If PACK 2(1) is set to On:
PACK 2(1)  OFF
Wait  60
PACK 2(1)  ON
If PACK 2(1) is set to OFF:
PACK 2(1)  ON
This will clear the
AIR  PACK 2(1) REGUL FAULT
alert. The
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED ABNORM PR
alert will remain.
Do not attempt to clear the
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED ABNORM PR
alert by applying the associated reset procedure.
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT
If the  BLEED NOT CLOSED  subtitle is
displayed in the  AIR
ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT  alert, do not reset the affected ENG BLEED.
Do not attempt more than one
reset. However, if the first reset is not successful and the  AIR
ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT  alert occurred after
takeoff with  APU  bleed ON, a second reset may be attempted
when flight conditions permit and the aircraft is stabilized in level
flight.
In flight or on ground:
If the pack (non-affected side) is operative, and if the wing
anti-ice is Off:
ENG BLEED  (affected side)  OFF
If the  ENG BLEED  (affected side)  FAULT  is On:
Delay application of the reset until  FAULT  goes off.
If the  ENG BLEED  (affected side)  FAULT  is OFF:
X BLEED  AUTO
PACK (affected side)  ON
ENG BLEED  (affected side)  ON
Check that the affected engine bleed valve is open on the  BLEED .
If
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT
alert triggers
again or if the engine bleed valve (affected side) is not open on
the  BLEED  :
ENG BLEED  (affected side)  OFF
PACK (affected side)  OFF
X BLEED  OPEN
Record the ENG BLEED reset in
the logbook (successful or not successful).

AUTO FLT - CAT 3 DUAL INOP SYS is displayed without any associated
ECAM alert
If the  CAT 3 DUAL  INOP SYS is associated with another  ECAM  alert (in particular  NAV ADR FAULT  or  NAV IR FAULT ), it means that the root cause
is not an  ADR  or  IR  rejection by  FAC  or  FMGC . Consequently, a change of  AP  or a  FAC  reset will not clear the  CAT 3 DUAL  INOP SYS.

record  BSCU  reset in the logbook.

AIRCRAFT  STOP
PARK BRK
ON
TOWING BAR
CONFIRM DISCONNECTED

OFF

A/SKID & N/W STRG
ON
If not successful:

C/B M34 (HYD / BRK & STRG / SYS1 / CTL)  PULL

C/B M34 (HYD / BRK & STRG / SYS1 / CTL)  PUSH
C/B M36 (HYD / BRK & STRG / SYS2 / SPLY)  PUSH
C/B M35 (HYD / BRK & STRG / SYS2 / CTL)  PUSH
After a successful reset, resume normal operation.
After any  BSCU  reset:  1. Check brake efficiency  2. Record  BSCU  reset in the logbook.

DATALINK - ATSU Malfunction
The  ATSU  reset should
be attempted only if:
INVALID DATA  is displayed on the  DCDU , or
Key selection
has no effect on the  DCDU  or any of the  MCDU
ATSU  DATALINK submenus, or
ADS-C ,  CPDLC ,
D-ATIS  ,
DCL  ,
OCL  , or  AOC  are inoperative.
Do not reset the  ATSU  if  ADS-C ,  CPDLC ,
D-ATIS  ,
DCL  ,
OCL  , or  AOC  are inoperative
due to a loss of the datalink communication means ( ATC DATALINK COM NOT AVAILABLE  on the  DCDU ).
In flight or on ground:
On 121VU:
C/B K43 (ATSU 1)  PULL
C/B K42 (ATSU 1 / SWTG)  PULL
Wait  5
C/B K42 (ATSU 1 / SWTG)  PUSH
C/B K43 (ATSU 1)  PUSH
When the  ATSU  is
reset, the following connections are no longer active:
CPDLC :
The flight crew
should send a notification to  ATC  center(s) to re-establish
the  CPDLC  connection.
ADS-C :
The flight crew
must check that  ADS-C  is ARMED or ON
The flight crew
should contact  ATC  center(s) by voice to re-establish the  ADS-C  connection.
ATC  center(s) consider
that the  ADS-C  connection is still alive because the  ADS-C  does not send a disconnect message.
DATALINK - ATSU Malfunction
The  ATSU  reset should
be attempted only if:
INVALID DATA  is displayed on the  DCDU , or
Key selection
has no effect on the  DCDU  or any of the  MCDU
ATSU  DATALINK submenus, or
ADS-C ,  CPDLC ,
D-ATIS  ,
DCL  ,
OCL  , or  AOC  are inoperative.
Do not reset the  ATSU  if  ADS-C ,  CPDLC ,
D-ATIS  ,
DCL  ,
OCL  , or  AOC  are inoperative
due to a loss of the datalink communication means ( ATC DATALINK COM NOT AVAILABLE  on the  DCDU ).
In flight or on ground:
On 121VU:
C/B L16 (ATSU 1)  PULL
C/B L15 (ATSU 1 / SWTG)  PULL
Wait  5
C/B L15 (ATSU 1 / SWTG)  PUSH
C/B L16 (ATSU 1)  PUSH
When the  ATSU  is
reset, the following connections are no longer active:
CPDLC :
The flight crew
should send a notification to  ATC  center(s) to re-establish
the  CPDLC  connection.
ADS-C :
The flight crew
must check that  ADS-C  is ARMED or ON
The flight crew
should contact  ATC  center(s) by voice to re-establish the  ADS-C  connection.
ATC  center(s) consider
that the  ADS-C  connection is still alive because the  ADS-C  does not send a disconnect message.

the  CINS  system reset if the reset by the cabin crew is
not successful.

CINS RESET
PRESS

ENG 1(2)  FADEC SYS FAULT
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
Reset  EEC  only when
engine is shut down.
When  FADEC
C/B s are pulled,  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT ) heating automatically starts,
and stays on until  FADEC
C/B s are pushed again.
ADIRU  probes can
become hot due to the reset procedure:
Before reset
procedure, and to avoid any damage to the  ADIRU  probes,
make sure that protective covers are removed from  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT )
After reset
procedure, note that  ADIRU  probes may be hot. As a consequence,
inform ground personnel if needed.
If
ENG
1(2)  FADEC SYS FAULT
alert triggers after the associated  ENG MASTER  is
set to ON:
The
ENG 1(2)  FADEC SYS FAULT
alert is latched. As a result, a maintenance action is
due.
If
ENG
1  FADEC SYS FAULT
alert
triggers during the  FADEC  power-up and when the  ENG 1 MASTER  is still set to OFF:
ENG MODE
NORM
ENG 1 FADEC GND PWR
OFF
On 49VU:
C/B A04 (ENG1 / FADEC A / & EIU1)  PULL
On 121VU:
C/B R41 (ENG1 / FADEC B / & EIU1)  PULL
Wait  5
On 49VU:
C/B A04 (ENG1 / FADEC A / & EIU1)  PUSH
On 121VU:
C/B R41 (ENG1 / FADEC B / & EIU1)  PUSH
At the next  FADEC  power-up,
check that the alert does not trigger. If the alert triggers again,
do not attempt another reset, a maintenance action is due.
If
ENG
2  FADEC SYS FAULT
alert
triggers during the  FADEC  power-up and when the  ENG 2 MASTER  is still set to OFF:
ENG MODE
NORM

ENG 1(2)  FADEC SYS FAULT
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
Reset  EEC  only when
engine is shut down.
When  FADEC
C/B s are pulled,  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT ) heating automatically starts,
and stays on until  FADEC
C/B s are pushed again.
ADIRU  probes can
become hot due to the reset procedure:
Before reset
procedure, and to avoid any damage to the  ADIRU  probes,
make sure that protective covers are removed from  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT )
After reset
procedure, note that  ADIRU  probes may be hot. As a consequence,
inform ground personnel if needed.
If
ENG
1(2)  FADEC SYS FAULT
alert triggers after the associated  ENG MASTER  is
set to ON:

due.

ENG  DUAL COOLING - NOT AVAIL
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
Several resets may be necessary
to recover the engine dual cooling function. However, do not attempt
more than 6 resets.
During automatic engine start, when the
flight crew presses  ENG DUAL COOLING  to ON, spurious
ENG  DUAL COOLING - NOT AVAIL
alert may trigger,
even if the operational conditions are inside the dual cooling envelope
( ). In this case, to
recover the engine dual cooling function, apply the following reset
procedure:
If  ENG 1+2 MASTER s are OFF:
ENG DUAL COOLING
OFF
Wait  3
ENG DUAL COOLING
ON
Check DUAL COOLING
memo on  E/WD :
If
DUAL COOLING
green memo appears on the  E/WD , resume automatic
engine start procedure with dual cooling function as per  SOP
If
DUAL COOLING
amber memo and
ENG  DUAL COOLING - NOT AVAIL
alert remain displayed on  ECAM  after the reset
attempt(s), consider the dual cooling function as inoperative. Resume
automatic engine start as per  SOP  with single cooling performed
on each engine.
If  ENG 1(2) MASTER  is ON:
Reset not authorized.
Consider the dual cooling function as inoperative. Resume automatic
engine start as per  SOP  with single cooling performed on
each engine.

In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
This reset procedure is only
authorized if the  ECAM  alert triggers only on one side
(  ENG 1
EIU FAULT
or
ENG 2
EIU FAULT  ).
If
ENG
1
EIU FAULT
or
ENG 2
EIU FAULT
triggers after engine start, during
the selection of the  ENG MODE  from IGN to NORM:
ENG MODE
IGN
Wait  1
ENG MODE
NORM
If the  ECAM  alert disappears
after the reset, resume normal operation.
ENG 1(2)  FADEC IDENT FAULT
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
FADEC / EEC  reset is only authorized when the engine is shut down.
When  FADEC
C/B s are pulled,  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT ) heating automatically starts,
and stays on until  FADEC
C/B s are pushed again.
ADIRU  probes can
become hot due to the reset procedure:
Before reset
procedure, and to avoid any damage to the  ADIRU  probes,
make sure that protective covers are removed from  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT )
After reset
procedure, note that  ADIRU  probes may be hot. As a consequence,
inform ground personnel if needed.
If
ENG
1  FADEC IDENT FAULT
triggers during  FADEC  power-up:
On 121VU:
C/B R40 (ENG1 /
FADEC B / & EIU1)  PULL
Wait  5
C/B R40 (ENG1 /
FADEC B / & EIU1)  PUSH
Wait  10
On 49VU:
C/B A04 (ENG1 /
FADEC A / & EIU1)  PULL
Wait  5
C/B A04 (ENG1 /
FADEC A / & EIU1)  PUSH
After application of this reset
procedure, a  COOLING  indication with a displayed cooling time may appear on the  E/WD , even if engine cooling is not required. Normal operation can be
resumed when the displayed cooling time is over.
If not successful
Do not attempt another reset.
Maintenance action is due.
If
ENG
2  FADEC IDENT FAULT
triggers during  FADEC  power-up:
On 121VU:
C/B R41 (ENG2 / FADEC B / & EIU2)  PULL
Wait  5
C/B R41 (ENG2 / FADEC B / & EIU2)  PUSH
Wait  10
On 49VU:
C/B A05 (ENG2 / FADEC A / & EIU2)  PULL
Wait  5
C/B A05 (ENG2 / FADEC A / & EIU2)  PUSH
After application of this reset
procedure, a  COOLING  indication with a displayed cooling time may appear on the  E/WD , even if engine cooling is not required. Normal operation can be
resumed when the displayed cooling time is over.
If not successful
Do not attempt another reset.
Maintenance action is due.
F/CTL  SPLR FAULT
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
This reset procedure is authorized
only with both engines running.
Do not reset  SEC  in the case of dispatch with  MMEL  item  ELAC  1,  SEC  1,  SEC  2 or  SEC  3
Do not attempt
more than one reset.
SEC 1
OFF
Wait  3
SEC 1
ON
SEC 2
OFF
Wait  3
SEC 2
ON
SEC 3
OFF
Wait  3
SEC 3
ON
FLIGHT CONTROLS  CHECK
Record the  SEC  1,  SEC  2 and  SEC  3 reset in the logbook (successful
or not successful).
FUEL - Loss of fuel quantity indication
In flight or on ground:
On 49VU:
C/B A13 (FUEL / FQI / CHAN / 1)  PULL
On 121VU:
C/B M27 (FUEL / FQI / CHAN / 2)  PULL
C/B L26 (FUEL / FQI / CHAN / 1&2)  PULL
Wait  5
On 49VU:
C/B A13 (FUEL / FQI / CHAN / 1)  PUSH
On 121VU:
C/B M27 (FUEL / FQI / CHAN / 2)  PUSH
C/B L26 (FUEL / FQI / CHAN / 1&2)  PUSH
The fuel quantity
indication will be re-established within  1
The fuel leak
detection function will be lost for the remainder of the flight. The
flight crew must monitor the fuel quantity.

CAB PR EXCESS CAB ALT
This  OEB  is issued to remind
the flight crew to apply the
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB ALT
ECAM  procedure, even if not confirmed on the  CAB PRESS .
A320 family aircraft equipped with  CPC  20791-13AC or older.
2024-01-11T00:00:00  2024-01-11
Head of Airbus Flight Operations & Training Support
CAB PR EXCESS CAB ALT
ECAM Entry
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB
ALT
Procedure
Apply the
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB ALT
ECAM  procedure,
even if not confirmed on the  CAB PRESS .
CAB PR EXCESS CAB ALT - Explanation
Root cause
Due to an internal failure, the active
Cabin Pressure Controller ( CPC ) may display an erroneous
cabin altitude on the  CAB  PRESS , although within
normal range. The active  CPC  regulates the cabin pressure
based on this erroneous value.  The second  CPC  (on standby), that is not affected by the internal failure,
will detect the abnormal cabin altitude, and trigger the
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB ALT
alert when the conditions
are satisfied. However, the cabin altitude from the standby  CPC  are not displayed to the flight crew. On the  CAB PRESS , only the cabin pressure parameters from the active  CPC  is displayed with the erroneous value (within the normal range).
Operational consequence
Due to the erroneous cabin altitude (within normal range) that is
displayed on the  CAB PRESS , the flight crew may unduly
disregard the
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB ALT
alert, or delay the start of the emergency descent, when required.
ENG  - MAINTENANCE  ENG 2 EIU message on
STATUS page
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
This reset procedure is only
authorized before pushback and with both engines shutdown. Do not
attempt more than one reset.
When  EIU  2  C/B s are pulled,  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot) heating automatically starts, and stays on until
both  EIU  2  C/B s are pushed again.
ADIRU  probes can
become hot due to the reset procedure:
Before reset
procedure, and to avoid any damage to the  ADIRU  probes,
make sure that protective covers are removed from  ADIRU  probes ( AOA , static, Pitot and  TAT )
After reset
procedure, note that  ADIRU  probes may be hot. As a consequence,
inform ground personnel if needed.
Spurious  MAINTENANCE
ENG  2  EIU  message may trigger on  STATUS  during aircraft power-up.  Before pushback and with both engines shutdown, reset the  EIU  2 as follows:
ENG MODE
NORM
On 49VU:
C/B A05 (ENG2 / FADEC A / & EIU2)  PULL
On 121VU:
C/B Q40 (ENG2 / FADEC B / & EIU2)  PULL
Wait  10
On 49VU:
C/B A05 (ENG2 / FADEC A / & EIU2)  PUSH
On 121VU:
C/B Q40 (ENG2 / FADEC B / & EIU2)  PUSH
If the  MAINTENANCE
ENG  2  EIU  message disappears from the  STATUS  after the reset, resume normal operation.  If the  MAINTENANCE
ENG  2  EIU  message triggers again on  STATUS , do not attempt another reset. Consider
dispatch under  MEL  or contact maintenance.

AIR ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT or AIR ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT
This  OEB  is published in order to provide the procedures
to avoid a pressure lock-up in the air bleed ducts in case of Pressure
Regulating Valve in failed open position because of High Pressure
Valve broken seal.
All A319neo, A320neo and A321neo aircraft without MOD 175847 (G1611)
for CFM engines or MOD 175846 (G1610) for PW engines
2025-03-25T00:00:00  2025-03-25
Head of Airbus Flight Operations & Training Support
AIR ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT or AIR ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT
ECAM Entry
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT  , or
AIR  ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT
Procedure
AT ANY TIME of the procedure, if
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB
ALT
alert triggers, IMMEDIATELY APPLY:
CAB PR  EXCESS CAB
ALT
ECAM PROC
If
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT  :
BLEED
CHECK
For the affected system,
check:
The air temperature
The position
of the engine bleed valve
The air pressure.
If affected engine bleed air temperature below  250 :
If affected engine bleed valve indicated open (in line) in
amber AND associated engine bleed air pressure above  60 :
ENG BLEED (affected side)  OFF
WING ANTI ICE  OFF
X BLEED  OPEN
AVOID ICING CONDITIONS
If opposite PACK available:
PACK (affected side)  OFF
If
AIR  ENG 1 BLEED FAULT  :
APU  BLEED  OFF
DO NOT RESET ENG BLEED
(affected)
In all other cases:
ECAM
PROC
APPLY
If affected engine bleed air temperature above  250 :
ECAM
PROC
APPLY
If
AIR  ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT  :
BLEED
CHECK
Check the air pressure
of both engine bleeds.
If both engine bleeds air pressure below  60 :
ECAM
PROC
APPLY
If at least one engine bleed air pressure above  60 :
X BLEED  OPEN
Both ENG BLEED  OFF
APU  BLEED  OFF
WING ANTI ICE  OFF
AVOID ICING CONDITIONS
DES TO FL 100/MEA-MORA
When at or below  FL  100/MEA-MORA and CAB PR ΔP <  1 :
RAM AIR  ON
MAX  FL
100/MEA-MORA
DO NOT RESET BOTH
ENG BLEEDS
AIR ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT or AIR ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT - Explanation
Root Cause
An early degradation and rupture of the
clip of the High Pressure Valve ( HPV ) causes the  HPV  sealing ring to come away from the valve.  In this case, the  HPV  does
not entirely close as required, resulting in an excessive residual
leakage.
In addition,
the  HPV  sealing ring that is broken and missing may go
downstream and also prevent the engine bleed valve from closing correctly.
The high pressure bleed air may reach the bleed ducts of the bleed
air consumers. If all the bleed air consumers are OFF, a pressure
lock-up (increase of pressure in a closed volume) occurs with a possible
risk of damage to the bleed ducts.
Operational Consequence
In the case that high pressure air goes
downstream the engine bleed valve, the
AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED
FAULT
or
AIR  ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT
alert triggers.  The application
of the associated  ECAM  procedures requests to switch off
all the bleed air consumers of the affected side(s). In order to prevent
this situation, an alternative procedure must be applied in order
to ensure that at least one bleed air consumer remains on.
ENG 1(2)  FIRE DET FAULT
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
If
ENG
1  FIRE DET FAULT  :
On 49VU:
C/B A06 (ENGINE / 1 / FIRE DET / LOOP A)  PULL
On 121VU:
C/B Q38 (ENGINE / ENG1 / FIRE DET / LOOP B)  PULL
Wait  10
On 49VU:
C/B A06 (ENGINE / 1 / FIRE DET / LOOP A)  PUSH
Within  4 , on
121VU:
C/B Q38 (ENGINE / ENG1 / FIRE DET / LOOP B)  PUSH
Both loops should
be powered together in less than  4 .
Wait  1  before recovering normal operation of the engine 1 fire
detection system.
If
ENG
2  FIRE DET FAULT  :
On 49VU:
C/B A07 (ENGINE / 2 / FIRE DET / LOOP B)  PULL
On 121VU:
C/B Q39 (ENGINE / ENG2 / FIRE DET / LOOP A)  PULL
Wait  10
On 49VU:
C/B A07 (ENGINE / 2 / FIRE DET / LOOP B)  PUSH
Within  4 , on
121VU:
C/B Q39 (ENGINE
/ ENG2 / FIRE DET / LOOP A)  PUSH
Both loops should
be powered together in less than  4 .
Wait  1  before recovering normal operation of the engine 2 fire
detection system.

Do not attempt to reset  MMR 1 or  MMR 2 during the approach or  RNP
AR  operations.
If
NAV  FLS 1 FAULT
:
On 49VU:
C/B G12 (NAV / MMR / 1)  PULL
Wait  10
C/B G12 (NAV / MMR / 1)  PUSH
If
NAV  FLS 2 FAULT
:
On 121VU:
C/B K09 or L07 (COM NAV / MMR / 2)  PULL
Wait  10
C/B K09 or L07 (COM NAV / MMR / 2)  PUSH
On some aircraft, the  MMR 2  C/B  may be located in L07 instead of K09.
If
NAV  FLS 1+2 FAULT
:
On 49VU:
C/B G12 (NAV / MMR / 1)  PULL
Wait  10
C/B G12 (NAV / MMR / 1)  PUSH
On 121VU:
C/B K09 or L07 (COM NAV / MMR / 2)  PULL
Wait  10
C/B K09 or L07 (COM NAV / MMR / 2)  PUSH
On some aircraft, the  MMR 2  C/B  may be located in L07 instead of K09.

apply the reset of the  MMR s after the interference area.

AIR - MAINTENANCE TEMP CTL 1 and TEMP CTL 2 messages on  STATUS
ECAM Alert or System
Malfunction
System Reset Procedure
Affected System
AIR -  MAINTENANCE  TEMP CTL 1 and TEMP CTL 2 messages on  STATUS
procedure
ACSC
AIR - MAINTENANCE TEMP CTL 1 and TEMP CTL 2 messages on  STATUS
In flight:
Not authorized.
On ground:
Erroneous  MAINTENANCE  TEMP CTL 1 and TEMP CTL 2 messages may trigger on the  STATUS  during aircraft power-up. In this case,
apply the following procedure:
Check that there is no  CIDS
INOP
SYS  and no  MAINTENANCE  CIDS 1(2) message displayed on
the  STATUS .
PACK 1+2
OFF
Confirm that both
pack flow control valves are closed on the  BLEED .
On 122VU:
C/B W21 (TEMP CTL SYS2 / CHAN A / 28VDC)  PULL
C/B X21 (TEMP CTL SYS1 / CHAN A / 28VDC)  PULL
C/B Y21 (TEMP CTL SYS2 / CHAN B / 28VDC)  PULL
On 49VU:
C/B D08 (TEMP CTL SYS1 / CHAN B / 28VDC)  PULL
Wait  10
On 122VU:
C/B Y21 (TEMP CTL SYS2 / CHAN B / 28VDC)  PUSH
C/B X21 (TEMP CTL SYS1 / CHAN A / 28VDC)  PUSH
C/B W21 (TEMP CTL SYS2 / CHAN A / 28VDC)  PUSH
On 49VU:
C/B D08 (TEMP CTL SYS1 / CHAN B / 28VDC)  PUSH
PACK 1+2
AS RQRD
Confirm that the  MAINTENANCE  TEMP CTL 1 and TEMP CTL 2 messages are no longer
displayed on the  STATUS .
